## Regions

Main article: [Regions of Sydney](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Regions_of_Sydney)



Sydney area at night, facing west. [Wollongong](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wollongong) is bottom left, and the [Central Coast](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_Coast_\(New_South_Wales\)) is at the far right.

The [Greater Sydney Commission](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greater_Sydney_Commission) divides Sydney into three "cities" and five "districts" based on the 33 [LGAs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Local_government_areas_of_New_South_Wales) in the metropolitan area. The "metropolis of three cities" comprises *Eastern Harbour City*, *Central River City* and *Western Parkland City*.[^cite_note-175] The Australian Bureau of Statistics also includes City of Central Coast (the former Gosford City and Wyong Shire) as part of Greater Sydney for population counts,[^cite_note-176] adding 330,000 people.[^cite_note-177]

### Inner suburbs



Historical buildings in [Millers Point](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Millers_Point), an inner suburb north of the CBD



Interior of the Victorian [Strand Arcade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Strand_Arcade)

The [CBD](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sydney_central_business_district) extends about 3 km (1.9 mi) south from [Sydney Cove](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sydney_Cove). It is bordered by [Farm Cove](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Farm_Cove,_New_South_Wales) within the [Royal Botanic Garden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Royal_Botanic_Garden,_Sydney) to the east and [Darling Harbour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Darling_Harbour) to the west. Suburbs surrounding the CBD include [Woolloomooloo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Woolloomooloo) and [Potts Point](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Potts_Point) to the east, [Surry Hills](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Surry_Hills) and [Darlinghurst](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Darlinghurst) to the south, [Pyrmont](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pyrmont,_New_South_Wales) and [Ultimo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ultimo,_New_South_Wales) to the west, and [Millers Point](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Millers_Point) and [The Rocks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Rocks,_New_South_Wales) to the north. Most of these suburbs measure less than 1 km<sup>2</sup> (0.4 sq mi) in area. The Sydney CBD is characterised by narrow streets and thoroughfares, created in its convict beginnings.[^cite_note-178]

Several localities, distinct from suburbs, exist throughout Sydney's inner reaches. [Central](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_railway_station,_Sydney) and [Circular Quay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Circular_Quay) are transport hubs with ferry, rail, and bus interchanges. [Chinatown](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinatown,_Sydney), Darling Harbour, and [Kings Cross](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kings_Cross,_New_South_Wales) are important locations for culture, tourism, and recreation. The [Strand Arcade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Strand_Arcade), located between [Pitt Street Mall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pitt_Street_Mall) and [George Street](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George_Street,_Sydney), is a historical [Victorian-style](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Victorian_architecture) shopping [arcade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arcade_\(architecture\)). Opened on 1 April 1892, its shop fronts are an exact replica of the original internal shopping facades.[^cite_note-179] [Westfield Sydney](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Westfield_Sydney), located beneath the [Sydney Tower](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sydney_Tower), is the largest shopping centre by area in Sydney.[^cite_note-180]

Since the late 20th century, there has been a trend of [gentrification](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gentrification) amongst Sydney's inner suburbs. Pyrmont, located on the harbour, was redeveloped from a centre of shipping and international trade to an area of [high density housing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/High_density_housing), tourist accommodation, and gambling.[^cite_note-181] Originally located well outside of the city, Darlinghurst is the location of the historic [Darlinghurst Gaol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Darlinghurst_Gaol), manufacturing, and mixed housing. For a period it was known as an area of prostitution. The terrace-style housing has largely been retained and Darlinghurst has undergone significant gentrification since the 1980s.[^cite_note-182][^cite_note-183][^cite_note-Darlinghurst-184]

[Green Square](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Green_Square,_New_South_Wales) is a former industrial area of [Waterloo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Waterloo,_New_South_Wales) which is undergoing urban renewal worth \$8 billion. On the city harbour edge, the historic suburb and wharves of Millers Point are being built up as the new area of [Barangaroo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barangaroo,_New_South_Wales).[^cite_note-185][^cite_note-186] The suburb of [Paddington](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paddington,_New_South_Wales) is known for its restored [terrace houses](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Terraced_house), [Victoria Barracks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Victoria_Barracks,_Sydney), and shopping including the weekly Oxford Street markets.[^cite_note-187]

### Inner West



[Newtown](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Newtown,_New_South_Wales) is one of the most complete [Victorian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Victorian_architecture) and [Edwardian era](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Edwardian_architecture) commercial precincts in Australia.

The [Inner West](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Inner_West) generally includes the [Inner West Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Inner_West_Council), [Municipality of Burwood](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Municipality_of_Burwood), [Municipality of Strathfield](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Municipality_of_Strathfield), and [City of Canada Bay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/City_of_Canada_Bay). These span up to about 11 km west of the CBD. Historically, especially prior to the building of the Harbour Bridge,[^cite_note-188] the outer suburbs of the Inner West such as [Strathfield](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Strathfield,_New_South_Wales) were the location of "country" estates for the colony's elites. By contrast, the inner suburbs in the Inner West, being close to transport and industry, have historically housed working-class industrial workers. These areas have undergone [gentrification](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gentrification) in the late 20th century, and many parts are now highly valued residential suburbs.[^cite_note-189] As of 2021, an Inner West suburb (Strathfield) remained one of the 20 most expensive postcodes in Australia by median house price (the others were all in metropolitan Sydney, all in Northern Sydney or the Eastern Suburbs).[^cite_note-top20-190] The [University of Sydney](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Sydney) is located in this area, as well as the [University of Technology Sydney](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Technology_Sydney) and a campus of the [Australian Catholic University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australian_Catholic_University). The Anzac Bridge spans Johnstons Bay and connects [Rozelle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rozelle) to [Pyrmont](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pyrmont,_New_South_Wales) and the city, forming part of the [Western Distributor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_Distributor_\(Sydney\)).

The Inner West is today well known as the location of village commercial centres with cosmopolitan flavours, such as the "Little Italy" commercial centres of Leichhardt, Five Dock and Haberfield,[^cite_note-191] "Little Portugal" in Petersham,[^cite_note-192] "Little Korea" in Strathfield[^cite_note-193] or "Little Shanghai" in Ashfield.[^cite_note-194] Large-scale shopping centres in the area include [Westfield Burwood](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Westfield_Burwood), [DFO Homebush](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/DFO_Homebush) and [Birkenhead Point Outlet Centre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Birkenhead_Point_Outlet_Centre). There is a large cosmopolitan community and nightlife hub on [King Street, Newtown](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/King_Street,_Newtown).

The area is serviced by [Sydney Trains](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sydney_Trains)' [T1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/North_Shore_&_Western_Line) and [T2](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leppington_&_Inner_West_Line), including the [Main Suburban Line](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Main_Suburban_railway_line), which was the first to be constructed in New South Wales. The [L1 light rail line](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Inner_West_Light_Rail) also runs through the area. [Strathfield railway station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Strathfield_railway_station) is a secondary railway hub within Sydney, and major station on the Suburban and [Northern](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Main_North_railway_line) lines. It was constructed in 1876.[^cite_note-195] The future [Sydney Metro West](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sydney_Metro_West) will also connect this area with the City and Parramatta. The area is also serviced by the [Parramatta River services](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parramatta_River_ferry_services) of [Sydney Ferries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sydney_Ferries),[^cite_note-196] numerous bus routes and cycleways.[^cite_note-197]

### Eastern Suburbs



Residences in [Bellevue Hill](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bellevue_Hill,_New_South_Wales). Sydney's eastern suburbs are made up of some of the most expensive real estate in the country.

[^cite_note-198]

The Eastern Suburbs encompass the [Municipality of Woollahra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Municipality_of_Woollahra), the [City of Randwick](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/City_of_Randwick), the [Waverley Municipal Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Waverley_Municipal_Council), and parts of the [Bayside Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bayside_Council). They include some of the most affluent and advantaged areas in the country, with some streets being amongst the most expensive in the world. As at 2014, [Wolseley Road](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wolseley_Road), [Point Piper](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Point_Piper), had a top price of \$20,900 per square metre, making it the ninth-most expensive street in the world.[^cite_note-199] More than 75% of neighbourhoods in the [Electoral District of Wentworth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Division_of_Wentworth) fall under the top decile of SEIFA advantage, making it the least disadvantaged area in the country.[^cite_note-200] As of 2021, of the 20 most expensive postcodes in Australia by median house price, nine were in the Eastern Suburbs.[^cite_note-top20-190]

Major landmarks include [Bondi Beach](368567.md), which was added to the [Australian National Heritage List](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australian_National_Heritage_List) in 2008;[^cite_note-201] and [Bondi Junction](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bondi_Junction), featuring a [Westfield shopping centre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Westfield_Bondi_Junction) and an estimated office workforce of 6,400 by 2035,[^cite_note-202] as well as a [railway station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bondi_Junction_railway_station) on the [T4 Eastern Suburbs Line](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eastern_Suburbs_railway_line). The suburb of [Randwick](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Randwick) contains [Randwick Racecourse](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Randwick_Racecourse), the [Royal Hospital for Women](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Royal_Hospital_for_Women), the [Prince of Wales Hospital](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prince_of_Wales_Hospital_\(Sydney\)), [Sydney Children's Hospital](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sydney_Children's_Hospital), and [University of New South Wales Kensington Campus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_New_South_Wales).[^cite_note-203]

Construction of the [CBD and South East Light Rail](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CBD_and_South_East_Light_Rail) was completed in April 2020.[^cite_note-204] The project aims to provide reliable and high-capacity tram services to residents in the City and South-East.

Major shopping centres in the area include [Westfield Bondi Junction](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Westfield_Bondi_Junction) and [Westfield Eastgardens](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Westfield_Eastgardens).

### Southern Sydney



[Kurnell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kurnell,_New_South_Wales), [La Perouse](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/La_Perouse,_New_South_Wales), and [Cronulla](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cronulla,_New_South_Wales), along with various other suburbs, face Botany Bay.

The Southern district of Sydney includes the suburbs in the [local government areas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Local_government_in_Australia) of the [Georges River Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Georges_River_Council) (collectively known as [St George](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/St_George,_New_South_Wales)) and the [Sutherland Shire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sutherland_Shire) (colloquially known as 'The Shire'), on the southern banks of the [Georges River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Georges_River).

The [Kurnell peninsula](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cronulla_dunes), near [Botany Bay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Botany_Bay), is the site of the first landfall on the eastern coastline made by James Cook in 1770. [La Perouse](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/La_Perouse,_New_South_Wales), a historic suburb named after the French navigator [Jean-François de Galaup, comte de Lapérouse](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jean-Fran%C3%A7ois_de_Galaup,_comte_de_Lap%C3%A9rouse), is notable for its old military outpost at [Bare Island](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bare_Island_\(New_South_Wales\)) and the [Botany Bay National Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Botany_Bay_National_Park).

The suburb of [Cronulla](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cronulla,_New_South_Wales) in [southern Sydney](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southern_Sydney) is close to Royal National Park, Australia's oldest national park. Hurstville, a large suburb with commercial and high-rise residential buildings dominating the skyline, has become a CBD for the southern suburbs.[^cite_note-205]

### Northern Sydney

Further information: [Northern Sydney](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northern_Sydney)



[Chatswood](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chatswood,_New_South_Wales) is a major commercial district.

'[Northern Sydney](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northern_Sydney)' includes the suburbs in the [Upper North Shore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Upper_North_Shore), [Lower North Shore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lower_North_Shore_\(Sydney\)) and the [Northern Beaches](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northern_Beaches).

The Northern Suburbs include several landmarks – [Macquarie University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Macquarie_University), [Gladesville Bridge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gladesville_Bridge), [Ryde Bridge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ryde_Bridge), [Macquarie Centre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Macquarie_Centre) and Curzon Hall in [Marsfield](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marsfield,_New_South_Wales). This area includes suburbs in the [local government areas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Local_government_in_Australia) of [Hornsby Shire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hornsby_Shire), [Ku-ring-gai Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ku-ring-gai_Council), [City of Ryde](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/City_of_Ryde), the [Municipality of Hunter's Hill](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Municipality_of_Hunter's_Hill) and parts of the [City of Parramatta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/City_of_Parramatta).

The North Shore includes the commercial centres of [North Sydney](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/North_Sydney,_New_South_Wales) and Chatswood. North Sydney itself consists of a large commercial centre, which contains the second largest concentration of high-rise buildings in Sydney after the CBD. North Sydney is dominated by advertising, marketing and associated trades, with many large corporations holding offices.

The Northern Beaches area includes [Manly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manly,_New_South_Wales), one of Sydney's most popular holiday destinations. The region also features [Sydney Heads](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sydney_Heads), a series of [headlands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Headlands) which form the entrance to Sydney Harbour. The Northern Beaches area extends south to the entrance of Port Jackson (Sydney Harbour), west to [Middle Harbour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Middle_Harbour) and north to the entrance of [Broken Bay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Broken_Bay).[^cite_note-206] The 2021 Australian census found the Northern Beaches to have, in comparison with the rest of Sydney, a large British diaspora and high concentration of people with European ancestry.[^cite_note-207]

As of the end of 2021, half of the 20 most expensive postcodes in Australia (by median house price) were in Northern Sydney, including four on the Northern Beaches, two on the Lower North Shore, three on the Upper North Shore, and one straddling [Hunters Hill](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hunters_Hill) and [Woolwich](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Woolwich,_New_South_Wales).[^cite_note-top20-190]

### Hills district

The [Hills district](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hills_District,_New_South_Wales) generally refers to the suburbs in north-western Sydney including the local government areas of [The Hills Shire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Hills_Shire), parts of the [City of Parramatta Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/City_of_Parramatta_Council) and [Hornsby Shire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hornsby_Shire). Actual suburbs and localities that are considered to be in the Hills District can be somewhat amorphous. For example, the Hills District Historical Society restricts its definition to the Hills Shire local government area, yet its study area extends from Parramatta to the Hawkesbury. The region is so named for its characteristically comparatively hilly topography as the Cumberland Plain lifts up, joining the Hornsby Plateau. [Windsor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Windsor_Road) and [Old Windsor Roads](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Old_Windsor_Road) are the second and third roads, respectively, laid in Australia.[^cite_note-208]

On 26 May 2019, The [Sydney Metro Northwest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sydney_Metro_Northwest), which went from Chatswood to Tallawong, opened, with a large portion running through the Hills District, which meant the Hills District, for the first time, started having heavy rail.[^cite_note-209] Before this, The Hills was served by [Bus Rapid Transit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bus_Rapid_Transit).

### Western suburbs

Further information: [Greater Western Sydney](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greater_Western_Sydney)

The greater western suburbs encompasses the areas of Parramatta, the sixth largest business district in Australia, settled the same year as the harbour-side colony,[^cite_note-210] [Bankstown](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bankstown), Liverpool, [Penrith](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Penrith,_New_South_Wales), and [Fairfield](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fairfield,_New_South_Wales). Covering 5,800 km<sup>2</sup> (2,200 sq mi) and having an estimated population as at 2017 of 2,288,554, western Sydney has the most [multicultural suburbs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minority_majority) in the country – [Cabramatta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cabramatta) has earned the nickname "[Little Saigon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Little_Saigon)" due to its [Vietnamese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vietnamese_Australians) population, Fairfield has been named "Little [Assyria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Assyria)" for its predominant [Assyrian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Assyrian_Australians) population and [Harris Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Harris_Park) is known as "[Little India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Little_India)" with its plurality of [Indian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_Australians) and [Hindu population](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hinduism_in_Australia).[^cite_note-211][^cite_note-herald-212][^cite_note-213][^cite_note-214] The population is predominantly of a working class background, with major employment in the [heavy industries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Heavy_industries) and [vocational](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vocational) trade.[^cite_note-215] Toongabbie is noted for being the third mainland settlement (after Sydney and Parramatta) set up after British colonisation began in 1788, although the site of the settlement is actually in the separate suburb of [Old Toongabbie](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Old_Toongabbie).[^cite_note-216]

The western suburb of [Prospect](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prospect,_New_South_Wales), in the [City of Blacktown](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/City_of_Blacktown), is home to [Sydney Action Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sydney_Action_Park), a [water park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Water_park) operated by [Jamberoo Action Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jamberoo_Action_Park).[^cite_note-217] [Auburn Botanic Gardens](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Auburn_Botanic_Gardens), a botanical garden in [Auburn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Auburn,_New_South_Wales), attracts thousands of visitors each year, including many from outside Australia.[^cite_note-chahgovau-218] The greater west also includes [Sydney Olympic Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sydney_Olympic_Park), a suburb created to host the 2000 Summer Olympics, and [Sydney Motorsport Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sydney_Motorsport_Park), a [circuit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Race_track) in [Eastern Creek](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eastern_Creek,_New_South_Wales).[^cite_note-219] [Prospect Hill](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prospect_Hill_\(New_South_Wales\)), a historically significant ridge in the west and the only area in Sydney with ancient [volcanic activity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Volcanic_activity),[^cite_note-220] is also listed on the State Heritage Register.[^cite_note-221]

To the northwest, [Featherdale Wildlife Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Featherdale_Wildlife_Park), a zoo in [Doonside](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Doonside,_New_South_Wales), near [Blacktown](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blacktown), is a major [tourist attraction](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tourist_attraction).[^cite_note-222] [Sydney Zoo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sydney_Zoo), opened in 2019, is another prominent zoo situated in [Bungaribee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bungaribee).[^cite_note-223] Established in 1799, the [Old Government House](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Old_Government_House,_Parramatta), a [historic house museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Historic_house_museum) and [tourist spot](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tourist_spot) in Parramatta, was included in the [Australian National Heritage List](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australian_National_Heritage_List) on 1 August 2007 and [World Heritage List](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Heritage_List) in 2010 (as part of the 11 penal sites constituting the [Australian Convict Sites](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australian_Convict_Sites)), making it the only site in greater western Sydney to be featured in such lists.[^cite_note-CourierMail01-224] The house is Australia's oldest surviving public building.[^cite_note-225]

Further to the southwest is the region of Macarthur and the city of [Campbelltown](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Campbelltown,_New_South_Wales), a significant population centre until the 1990s considered a region separate to Sydney proper. [Macarthur Square](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Macarthur_Square), a shopping complex in Campbelltown, has become one of the largest shopping complexes in Sydney.[^cite_note-226] The southwest also features [Bankstown Reservoir](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bankstown_Reservoir), the oldest elevated reservoir constructed in [reinforced concrete](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reinforced_concrete) that is still in use and is listed on the State Heritage Register.[^cite_note-227] The southwest is home to one of Sydney's oldest trees, the [Bland Oak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bland_Oak), which was planted in the 1840s by [William Bland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/William_Bland) in [Carramar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carramar,_New_South_Wales).[^cite_note-mobbaymag-228]

## Urban structure



The [Sydney CBD](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sydney_central_business_district) with the [Opera House](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sydney_Opera_House) and [Harbour Bridge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sydney_Harbour_Bridge). Sydney is home to the most high-rise buildings in the nation.

[^cite_note-229]

### Architecture

See also: [Architecture of Sydney](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Architecture_of_Sydney), [List of heritage houses in Sydney](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_heritage_houses_in_Sydney), and [List of tallest buildings in Sydney](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_tallest_buildings_in_Sydney)

The earliest structures in the colony were built to the bare minimum of standards. Governor Macquarie set ambitious targets for the design of new construction projects. The city now has a world heritage listed building, several national heritage listed buildings, and dozens of Commonwealth heritage listed buildings as evidence of the survival of Macquarie's ideals.[^cite_note-230][^cite_note-231][^cite_note-232]



[York Street](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/York_Street,_Sydney) is an example of a city street in Sydney with an array of intact Victorian heritage architecture.

In 1814, the governor called on a convict named [Francis Greenway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Francis_Greenway) to design [Macquarie Lighthouse](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Macquarie_Lighthouse).[^cite_note-Macquarie_Lighthouse-233] The lighthouse's [Classical](52687.md) design earned Greenway a pardon from Macquarie in 1818 and introduced a culture of refined architecture that remains to this day.[^cite_note-Macquarie_Lightstation-234] Greenway went on to design the [Hyde Park Barracks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hyde_Park_Barracks,_Sydney) in 1819 and the [Georgian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Georgian_architecture) style [St James's Church](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/St_James'_Church,_Sydney) in 1824.[^cite_note-235][^cite_note-236] [Gothic-inspired architecture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gothic_architecture) became more popular from the 1830s. [John Verge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Verge)'s [Elizabeth Bay House](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elizabeth_Bay_House) and [St Philip's Church](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/St_Philip's_Church,_Sydney) of 1856 were built in [Gothic Revival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gothic_Revival_architecture) style along with [Edward Blore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Edward_Blore)'s [Government House](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Government_House,_Sydney) of 1845.[^cite_note-Chronology_of_styles_in_Australian_architecture-237][^cite_note-238] Kirribilli House, completed in 1858, and St Andrew's Cathedral, Australia's oldest cathedral,[^cite_note-239] are rare examples of Victorian Gothic construction.[^cite_note-Chronology_of_styles_in_Australian_architecture-237][^cite_note-240]



[General Post Office](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_Post_Office,_Sydney)

From the late 1850s there was a shift towards Classical architecture. [Mortimer Lewis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mortimer_Lewis) designed the [Australian Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australian_Museum) in 1857.[^cite_note-241] The [General Post Office](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_Post_Office,_Sydney), completed in 1891 in [Victorian Free Classical](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Victorian_architecture) style, was designed by [James Barnet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Barnet).[^cite_note-242] Barnet also oversaw the 1883 reconstruction of Greenway's Macquarie Lighthouse.[^cite_note-Macquarie_Lighthouse-233][^cite_note-Macquarie_Lightstation-234] [Customs House](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Customs_House,_Sydney) was built in 1844.[^cite_note-243] The neo-Classical and [French Second Empire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Second_Empire_architecture) style [Town Hall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sydney_Town_Hall) was completed in 1889.[^cite_note-244][^cite_note-245] [Romanesque](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romanesque_architecture) designs gained favour from the early 1890s. [Sydney Technical College](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sydney_Technical_College) was completed in 1893 using both Romanesque Revival and [Queen Anne](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Queen_Anne_style_architecture) approaches.[^cite_note-Sydney_Technical_College-246] The [Queen Victoria Building](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Queen_Victoria_Building) was designed in Romanesque Revival fashion by [George McRae](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George_McRae); completed in 1898,[^cite_note-247] it accommodates 200 shops across its three storeys.[^cite_note-248]

As the wealth of the settlement increased and Sydney developed into a metropolis after Federation in 1901, its buildings became taller. Sydney's first tower was Culwulla Chambers which topped out at 50 m (160 ft) making 12 floors. The Commercial Traveller's Club, built in 1908, was of similar height at 10 floors. It was built in a brick stone veneer and demolished in 1972.[^cite_note-249] This heralded a change in Sydney's cityscape and with the lifting of height restrictions in the 1960s there came a surge of high-rise construction.[^cite_note-Sydney_architecture-250]

The Great Depression had a tangible influence on Sydney's architecture. New structures became more restrained with far less ornamentation. The most notable architectural feat of this period is the Harbour Bridge. Its steel arch was designed by [John Bradfield](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Bradfield_\(engineer\)) and completed in 1932. A total of 39,000 tonnes of structural steel span the 503 m (1,650 ft) between Milsons Point and [Dawes Point](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dawes_Point,_New_South_Wales).[^cite_note-Sydney_Harbour_Bridge-251][^cite_note-252]



[Frank Gehry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Frank_Gehry)'s [Dr Chau Chak Wing Building](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dr_Chau_Chak_Wing_Building)

[Modern](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Modern_architecture) and [International architecture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_architecture) came to Sydney from the 1940s. Since its completion in 1973 the city's Opera House has become a World Heritage Site and one of the world's most renowned pieces of Modern design. [Jørn Utzon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/J%C3%B8rn_Utzon) was awarded the [Pritzker Prize](53422.md) in 2003 for his work on the Opera House.[^cite_note-Sydney_Opera_House-253] Sydney is home to Australia's first building by renowned Canadian-American architect [Frank Gehry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Frank_Gehry), the [Dr Chau Chak Wing Building](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dr_Chau_Chak_Wing_Building) (2015). An entrance from [The Goods Line](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Goods_Line)–a pedestrian pathway and former railway line–is located on the eastern border of the site.

Contemporary buildings in the CBD include [Citigroup Centre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Citigroup_Centre,_Sydney),[^cite_note-254] [Aurora Place](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aurora_Place),[^cite_note-255] [Chifley Tower](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chifley_Tower),[^cite_note-256][^cite_note-257] the [Reserve Bank](492355.md) building,[^cite_note-258] [Deutsche Bank Place](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deutsche_Bank_Place),[^cite_note-259] [MLC Centre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MLC_Centre),[^cite_note-260] and [Capita Centre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Capita_Centre).[^cite_note-261] The tallest structure is [Sydney Tower](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sydney_Tower), designed by Donald Crone and completed in 1981.[^cite_note-262] Due to the proximity of [Sydney Airport](266313.md), a maximum height restriction was imposed, now sitting at 330 metres (1083 feet).[^cite_note-263] [Green bans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Green_ban) and [heritage overlays](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Heritage_overlay) have been in place since at least 1977 to protect Sydney's heritage after controversial demolitions in the 1970s.[^cite_note-264]

### Housing



[Terraces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Terrace_houses_in_Australia) in [Kirribilli](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kirribilli)

Sydney surpasses both New York City and Paris real estate prices, having some of the most expensive in the world.[^cite_note-265][^cite_note-266] The city remains Australia's most expensive housing market, with the median house price at \$1,751,728 as of October 2025.[^cite_note-267]

There were 1.83 million dwellings in Sydney in 2021 including 900,000 (54%) detached houses, 218,000 (13%) semi-detached terrace houses and 550,000 (33%) units and apartments.[^cite_note-Greater_Sydney_QuickStats-268] Whilst terrace houses are common in the inner city areas, detached houses dominate the landscape in the outer suburbs. Due to environmental and economic pressures, there has been a noted trend towards denser housing, with a 30% increase in the number of apartments between 1996 and 2006.[^cite_note-Housing_Sydney-269] Public housing in Sydney is managed by the [Government of New South Wales](1211428.md).[^cite_note-270] Suburbs with large concentrations of public housing include [Claymore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Claymore,_New_South_Wales), [Macquarie Fields](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Macquarie_Fields), [Waterloo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Waterloo,_New_South_Wales), and [Mount Druitt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mount_Druitt).

A range of heritage housing styles can be found throughout Sydney. Terrace houses are found in the inner suburbs such as [Paddington](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paddington,_New_South_Wales), [The Rocks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Rocks,_New_South_Wales), [Potts Point](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Potts_Point) and [Balmain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Balmain,_New_South_Wales), many of which have been the subject of [gentrification](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gentrification).[^cite_note-271][^cite_note-272] These terraces, particularly those in suburbs such as The Rocks, were historically home to Sydney's miners and labourers. In the present day, terrace houses now make up some of the most valuable real estate in the city.[^cite_note-273] Surviving large mansions from the Victorian era are mostly found in the oldest suburbs, such as [Double Bay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Double_Bay), [Darling Point](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Darling_Point), [Rose Bay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rose_Bay,_New_South_Wales) and [Strathfield](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Strathfield).[^cite_note-274]

[Federation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Federation_architecture) homes, constructed around the time of Federation in 1901, are located in a large number of suburbs that developed thanks to the arrival of railways in the late 19th century, such as [Penshurst](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Penshurst,_New_South_Wales) and [Turramurra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turramurra), and in large-scale planned "garden suburbs" such as [Haberfield](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Haberfield,_New_South_Wales). Workers cottages are found in [Surry Hills](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Surry_Hills), [Redfern](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Redfern,_New_South_Wales), and Balmain. [California bungalows](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/California_bungalow) are common in [Ashfield](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ashfield,_New_South_Wales), [Concord](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Concord,_New_South_Wales), and [Beecroft](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beecroft,_New_South_Wales). Larger modern homes are predominantly found in the outer suburbs, such as [Stanhope Gardens](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stanhope_Gardens,_New_South_Wales), [Kellyville Ridge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kellyville_Ridge), [Bella Vista](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bella_Vista,_New_South_Wales) to the northwest, [Bossley Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bossley_Park), [Abbotsbury](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abbotsbury,_New_South_Wales), and [Cecil Hills](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cecil_Hills) to the west, and [Hoxton Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hoxton_Park), [Harrington Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Harrington_Park,_New_South_Wales), and [Oran Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oran_Park,_New_South_Wales) to the southwest.[^cite_note-275]

### Parks and open spaces

Main article: [Parks in Sydney](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parks_in_Sydney)



The [Centennial Parklands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Centennial_Parklands) is the largest park in the City of Sydney, comprising 189 ha (470 acres).

[^cite_note-CPMPT_cp-276]



The [Anzac War Memorial](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anzac_War_Memorial) in [Hyde Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hyde_Park,_Sydney) is a public memorial dedicated to the achievement of the [Australian Imperial Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_Australian_Imperial_Force) of [World War I](4764461.md).

[^cite_note-277]

The [Anzac War Memorial](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anzac_War_Memorial) in [Hyde Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hyde_Park,_Sydney) is a public memorial dedicated to the [Australian Imperial Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_Australian_Imperial_Force) of [World War I](4764461.md).

The [Royal Botanic Garden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Royal_Botanic_Gardens,_Sydney) is the most iconic green space in the region, hosting both scientific and leisure activities.[^cite_note-278] There are 15 separate parks under the City administration.[^cite_note-279] Parks within the city centre include [Hyde Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hyde_Park,_Sydney), [The Domain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Domain,_Sydney) and Prince Alfred Park.

The [Centennial Parklands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Centennial_Parklands) is the largest park in the City of Sydney, comprising 189 ha (470 acres).

The inner suburbs include [Centennial Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Centennial_Park,_New_South_Wales) and [Moore Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moore_Park,_New_South_Wales) in the east (both within the City of Sydney local government area), while the outer suburbs contain [Sydney Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sydney_Park) and Royal National Park in the south, [Ku-ring-gai Chase National Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ku-ring-gai_Chase_National_Park) in the north, and [Western Sydney Parklands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_Sydney_Parklands) in the west, which is [one of the largest urban parks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_urban_parks_by_size) in the world. The Royal National Park was proclaimed in 1879 and with 13,200 ha (51 sq mi) is the second oldest national park in the world.[^cite_note-280]

Hyde Park is the oldest parkland in the country.[^cite_note-281] The largest park in the Sydney metropolitan area is Ku-ring-gai Chase National Park, established in 1894 with an area of 15,400 ha (59 sq mi).[^cite_note-282] It is regarded for its well-preserved records of indigenous habitation – more than 800 rock engravings, cave drawings and middens.[^cite_note-283]

The area now known as The Domain was set aside by Governor Arthur Phillip in 1788 as his private reserve.[^cite_note-Royal_Botanic_Gardens_history-284] Under the orders of Macquarie the land to the immediate north of The Domain became the Royal Botanic Garden in 1816. This makes them the oldest botanic garden in Australia.[^cite_note-Royal_Botanic_Gardens_history-284] The Gardens host scientific research with herbarium collections, a library and laboratories.[^cite_note-285] The two parks have a total area of 64 ha (0.2 sq mi) with 8,900 individual plant species and receive over 3.5 million annual visits.[^cite_note-286]

To the south of The Domain is Hyde Park, the oldest public parkland in Australia which measures 16.2 ha (0.1 sq mi).[^cite_note-287] Its location was used for both relaxation and [grazing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grazing) of animals from the earliest days of the colony.[^cite_note-288] Macquarie dedicated it in 1810 for the "recreation and amusement of the inhabitants of the town" and named it in honour of [Hyde Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hyde_Park,_London) in London.

## Economy

Main article: [Economy of Sydney](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economy_of_Sydney)



The [central business district](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sydney_central_business_district). Sydney is the financial and economic centre of Australia, having the largest economy and contributing a quarter of Australia's total [GDP.](12594.md)

[^cite_note-289]

Researchers from [Loughborough University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loughborough_University) have ranked Sydney amongst the top ten world cities that are highly integrated into the global economy.[^cite_note-290] The Global Economic Power Index ranks Sydney eleventh in the world.[^cite_note-291] The Global Cities Index recognises it as fourteenth in the world based on global engagement.[^cite_note-2014_Global_Cities_Index-292] There is a significant concentration of foreign banks and multinational corporations in Sydney and the city is promoted as Australia's financial capital and one of the [Asia–Pacific](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asia%E2%80%93Pacific)'s leading financial hubs.[^cite_note-Economic_powerhouse-293][^cite_note-Economic_profile-294]

The prevailing economic theory during early colonial days was [mercantilism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mercantilism), as it was throughout most of Western Europe.[^cite_note-Economy-295] The economy struggled at first due to difficulties in cultivating the land and the lack of a stable monetary system. Governor Macquarie created [two coins](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Holey_dollar) from every [Spanish silver dollar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spanish_dollar) in circulation.[^cite_note-Economy-295] The economy was [capitalist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Capitalism) in nature by the 1840s as the proportion of free settlers increased, the maritime and wool industries flourished, and the powers of the [East India Company](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/East_India_Company) were curtailed.[^cite_note-Economy-295]

Wheat, gold, and other minerals became export industries towards the end of the 1800s.[^cite_note-Economy-295] Significant capital began to flow into the city from the 1870s to finance roads, railways, bridges, docks, courthouses, schools and hospitals. [Protectionist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Protectionism) policies after [federation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Federation_of_Australia) allowed for the creation of a manufacturing industry which became the city's largest employer by the 1920s.[^cite_note-Economy-295] These same policies helped to relieve the effects of the Great Depression during which the unemployment rate in New South Wales reached as high as 32%.[^cite_note-Economy-295] From the 1960s onwards Parramatta gained recognition as the city's second CBD and finance and tourism became major industries and sources of employment.[^cite_note-Economy-295]

Sydney's nominal gross domestic product was AU\$490 [billion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1,000,000,000) ([US\$](18717338.md)368.42 [billion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1,000,000,000)) in 2021.[^cite_note-296] The financial and insurance services industry accounts for 18.1% of gross product, ahead of professional services with 9% and manufacturing with 7.2%. The creative and technology sectors are also focus industries for the City of Sydney and represented 9% and 11% of its economic output in 2012.[^cite_note-297][^cite_note-298]

### Businesses

There were 451,000 businesses based in Sydney in 2011, including 48% of the top 500 companies in Australia and two-thirds of the regional headquarters of multinational corporations.[^cite_note-299] Global companies are attracted to the city in part because its time zone spans the closing of business in North America and the opening of business in Europe. Most foreign companies in Sydney maintain significant sales and service functions but comparably less production, research, and development capabilities.[^cite_note-300] There are 283 multinational companies with regional offices in Sydney.[^cite_note-301]

### Domestic economics



[Pitt Street](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pitt_Street), a major street in the [CBD](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sydney_central_business_district), runs from [Circular Quay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Circular_Quay) in the north to [Waterloo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Waterloo,_New_South_Wales) in the south, and is home to many large high-end retailers.

[^cite_note-302]

Sydney has been ranked between the fifteenth and the fifth most expensive city in the world and is the most expensive city in Australia.[^cite_note-Prices_and_earnings-303] Of the 15 categories only measured by UBS in 2012, workers receive the seventh highest wage levels of 77 cities in the world.[^cite_note-Prices_and_earnings-303] Working residents of Sydney work an average of 1,846 hours per annum with 15 days of leave.[^cite_note-Prices_and_earnings-303]

The labour force of Greater Sydney Region in 2016 was 2,272,722 with a participation rate of 61.6%.[^cite_note-304] It comprised 61.2% full-time workers, 30.9% part-time workers, and 6.0% unemployed individuals.[^cite_note-Greater_Sydney_QuickStats-268][^cite_note-305] The largest reported occupations are professionals, clerical and administrative workers, managers, technicians and trades workers, and community and personal service workers.[^cite_note-Greater_Sydney_QuickStats-268] The largest industries by employment across Greater Sydney are Health Care and Social Assistance (11.6%), Professional Services (9.8%), Retail Trade (9.3%), Construction (8.2%), Education and Training (8.0%), Accommodation and Food Services (6.7%), and Financial and Insurance Services (6.6%).[^cite_note-ABSGCCSAXLS-3] The Professional Services and Financial and Insurance Services industries account for 25.4% of employment within the City of Sydney.[^cite_note-306]

In 2016, 57.6% of working-age residents had a weekly income of less than \$1,000 and 14.4% had a weekly income of \$1,750 or more.[^cite_note-307] The median weekly income for the same period was \$719 for individuals, \$1,988 for families, and \$1,750 for households.[^cite_note-308]

Unemployment in the City of Sydney averaged 4.6% for the decade to 2013, much lower than the current rate of unemployment in Western Sydney of 7.3%.[^cite_note-Economic_profile-294][^cite_note-The_daily_exodus_from_western_Sydney-309] Western Sydney continues to struggle to create jobs to meet its population growth despite the development of commercial centres like Parramatta. Each day about 200,000 commuters travel from Western Sydney to the CBD and suburbs in the east and north of the city.[^cite_note-The_daily_exodus_from_western_Sydney-309]

Home ownership in Sydney was less common than renting prior to the Second World War but this trend has since reversed.[^cite_note-Housing_Sydney-269] Median house prices have increased by an average of 8.6% per annum since 1970.[^cite_note-310][^cite_note-311] The median house price in March 2014 was \$630,000.[^cite_note-312] The primary cause of rising prices is the increasing cost of land and scarcity.[^cite_note-313] 31.6% of dwellings in Sydney are rented, 30.4% are owned outright and 34.8% are owned with a mortgage.[^cite_note-Greater_Sydney_QuickStats-268] 11.8% of mortgagees in 2011 had monthly loan repayments of less than \$1,000 and 82.9% had monthly repayments of \$1,000 or more.[^cite_note-ABSGCCSAXLS-3] 44.9% of renters for the same period had weekly rent of less than \$350 whilst 51.7% had weekly rent of \$350 or more. The median weekly rent in Sydney in 2011 was \$450.[^cite_note-ABSGCCSAXLS-3]

### Financial services



[State Savings Bank](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_Savings_Bank_building)

Macquarie gave a charter in 1817 to form the first bank in Australia, the [Bank of New South Wales](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bank_of_New_South_Wales).[^cite_note-Australia's_banking_history-314] New private banks opened throughout the 1800s but the financial system was unstable. Bank collapses were frequent and a crisis point was reached in 1893 when 12 banks failed.[^cite_note-Australia's_banking_history-314]

The Bank of New South Wales exists to this day as [Westpac](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Westpac).[^cite_note-315] The [Commonwealth Bank of Australia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Commonwealth_Bank) was formed in Sydney in 1911 and began to issue notes backed by the resources of the nation. It was replaced in this role in 1959 by the [Reserve Bank of Australia](492355.md), also based in Sydney.[^cite_note-Australia's_banking_history-314] The [Australian Securities Exchange](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australian_Securities_Exchange) began operating in 1987 and with a market capitalisation of \$1.6 trillion is now one of the ten largest exchanges in the world.[^cite_note-316]

The Financial and Insurance Services industry now constitutes 43% of the economic product of the City of Sydney.[^cite_note-Economic_powerhouse-293] Sydney makes up half of Australia's finance sector and has been promoted by consecutive Commonwealth Governments as the [Asia–Pacific](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asia%E2%80%93Pacific)'s leading financial centre.[^cite_note-Tough_week_for_a_Sydney_success_story-24][^cite_note-Another_shot_at_making_city_a_finance_hub-25][^cite_note-317] In the 2017 [Global Financial Centres Index](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Global_Financial_Centres_Index), Sydney was ranked as having the eighth most competitive financial centre in the world.[^cite_note-318]

In 1985 the Federal Government granted 16 banking licences to foreign banks and now 40 of the 43 foreign banks operating in Australia are based in Sydney, including the [People's Bank of China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/People's_Bank_of_China), [Bank of America](347756.md), [Citigroup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Citigroup), [UBS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UBS), [Mizuho Bank](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mizuho_Bank), [Bank of China](303789.md), [Banco Santander](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Banco_Santander), [Credit Suisse](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Credit_Suisse), [Standard Chartered](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Standard_Chartered), [State Street](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_Street_Corporation), [HSBC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/HSBC), [Deutsche Bank](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deutsche_Bank), [Barclays](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barclays), [Royal Bank of Canada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Royal_Bank_of_Canada), [Société Générale](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soci%C3%A9t%C3%A9_G%C3%A9n%C3%A9rale), [Royal Bank of Scotland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Royal_Bank_of_Scotland), [Sumitomo Mitsui](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sumitomo_Mitsui_Banking_Corporation), [ING Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ING_Group), [BNP Paribas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BNP_Paribas), and [Investec](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Investec).[^cite_note-Economic_powerhouse-293][^cite_note-Australia's_banking_history-314][^cite_note-319][^cite_note-320]

### Manufacturing

Main article: [Manufacturing in Australia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manufacturing_in_Australia)

Sydney has been a manufacturing city since the 1920s. By 1961 the industry accounted for 39% of all employment and by 1970 over 30% of all Australian manufacturing jobs were in Sydney.[^cite_note-Fitzgerald-2011a-321] Its status has declined in recent decades, making up 12.6% of employment in 2001 and 8.5% in 2011.[^cite_note-ABSGCCSAXLS-3][^cite_note-Fitzgerald-2011a-321] Between 1970 and 1985 there was a loss of 180,000 manufacturing jobs.[^cite_note-Fitzgerald-2011a-321] Despite this, Sydney still overtook Melbourne as the largest manufacturing centre in Australia in the 2010s,[^cite_note-322] with a manufacturing output of \$21.7 billion in 2013.[^cite_note-Sydney_takes_manufacturing_capital_crown_from_Melbourne-323] Observers have credited Sydney's focus on the domestic market and high-tech manufacturing for its resilience against the high Australian dollar of the early 2010s.[^cite_note-Sydney_takes_manufacturing_capital_crown_from_Melbourne-323] The *Smithfield-Wetherill Park Industrial Estate* in [Western Sydney](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greater_Western_Sydney) is the largest [industrial estate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Industrial_estate) in the Southern Hemisphere and is the centre of manufacturing and distribution in the region.[^cite_note-324]

### Tourism and international education

Main article: [Tourism in Sydney](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tourism_in_Sydney)



[Darling Harbour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Darling_Harbour) is a major entertainment and tourism precinct.&#x20;

Sydney is a gateway to Australia for many international visitors and ranks among the top sixty most visited cities in the world.[^cite_note-\:1-325] It has hosted over 2.8 million international visitors in 2013, or nearly half of all international visits to Australia. These visitors spent 59 million nights in the city and a total of \$5.9 billion.[^cite_note-Our_global_city-28] The countries of origin in descending order were China, New Zealand, the United Kingdom, the United States, South Korea, Japan, Singapore, Germany, Hong Kong, and India.[^cite_note-Travel_to_Sydney-326]

The city also received 8.3 million domestic overnight visitors in 2013 who spent a total of \$6 billion.[^cite_note-Travel_to_Sydney-326] 26,700 workers in the City of Sydney were directly employed by tourism in 2011.[^cite_note-Tourism-327] There were 480,000 visitors and 27,500 people staying overnight each day in 2012.[^cite_note-Tourism-327] On average, the tourism industry contributes \$36 million to the city's economy per day.[^cite_note-Tourism-327]

Popular destinations include the Sydney Opera House, the Sydney Harbour Bridge, [Watsons Bay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Watsons_Bay), [The Rocks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Rocks,_New_South_Wales), Sydney Tower, [Darling Harbour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Darling_Harbour), the Royal Botanic Garden, the [Australian Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australian_Museum), the [Museum of Contemporary Art](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Museum_of_Contemporary_Art_Australia), the [Art Gallery of New South Wales](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Art_Gallery_of_New_South_Wales), the [Queen Victoria Building](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Queen_Victoria_Building), [Sea Life Sydney Aquarium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sea_Life_Sydney_Aquarium), [Taronga Zoo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taronga_Zoo), [Bondi Beach](368567.md), [Luna Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luna_Park_Sydney) and [Sydney Olympic Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sydney_Olympic_Park).[^cite_note-328]

Major developmental projects designed to increase Sydney's tourism sector include a [casino and hotel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crown_Sydney) at Barangaroo and the redevelopment of [East Darling Harbour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Darling_Harbour#Redevelopment), which involves a new [exhibition and convention centre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Convention_Centre_Sydney), now Australia's largest.[^cite_note-329][^cite_note-330][^cite_note-beabeats.com.au-331]

Sydney is the highest-ranking city in the world for international students. More than 50,000 international students study at the city's universities and a further 50,000 study at its [vocational](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/TAFE_NSW) and English language schools.[^cite_note-2014_Global_Cities_Index-292][^cite_note-332] International education contributes \$1.6 billion to the local economy and creates demand for 4,000 local jobs each year.[^cite_note-333]

### Housing affordability

In 2023, Sydney was ranked the least affordable city to buy a house in Australia and the second least affordable city in the world, after Hong Kong,[^cite_note-334] with the average Sydney house price in late 2023 costing A\$1.59 million, and the average unit price costing A\$795,000.[^cite_note-335] As of early 2024, Sydney is often described in the media as having a housing shortage, or suffering a housing crisis.[^cite_note-336][^cite_note-337]

## Demographics

Main article: [Demographics of Sydney](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Demographics_of_Sydney)



[Chinese New Year](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_New_Year) celebrations in [Chinatown](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinatown,_Sydney). Sydney is home to the nation's largest population of [Chinese Australians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_Australians).

[^cite_note-338]

The population of Sydney in 1788 was less than 1,000.[^cite_note-Immigration-339] With convict transportation it almost tripled in ten years to 2,953.[^cite_note-Australian_historical_population_statistics-340] For each decade since 1961 the population has increased by more than 250,000.[^cite_note-341] The 2021 census recorded the population of Greater Sydney as 5,231,150.[^cite_note-ABSGCCSA-4] The Australian Treasury expects the population will grow to 6.5 million in 2033–34.[^cite_note-342] The four most densely populated suburbs in Australia are located in Sydney with each having more than 13,000 residents per square kilometre (33,700 residents per square mile).[^cite_note-343] Between 1971 and 2018, Sydney experienced a net loss of 716,832 people to the rest of Australia, but its population grew due to overseas arrivals and a healthy birth rate.[^cite_note-344]

The median age of Sydney residents is 37 and 14.8% of people are 65 or older.[^cite_note-Greater_Sydney_QuickStats-268] 48.6% of Sydney's population is married whilst 36.7% have never been married.[^cite_note-Greater_Sydney_QuickStats-268] 49.0% of families are couples with children, 34.4% are couples without children, and 14.8% are single-parent families.[^cite_note-Greater_Sydney_QuickStats-268]
