At the 2021 census, 40.5% of Sydney's population was born overseas. Foreign countries of birth with the greatest representation are mainland China, India, England, Vietnam, Philippines and New Zealand.[^cite_note-Australian-Bureau-of-Statistics-15]

At the 2021 census, 1.7% of Sydney's population identified as being [Indigenous](12598742.md) — [Aboriginal Australians](2912594.md) and [Torres Strait Islanders](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Torres_Strait_Islanders).[^cite_note-349][^cite_note-Abs.gov.au-350]

### Language

42% of households in Sydney use a language other than English, with the most common being Mandarin (5%), Arabic (4.2%), Cantonese (2.8%), Vietnamese (2.2%) and [Hindi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hindi) (1.5%).[^cite_note-Abs.gov.au-350]

### Religion



[St Mary's Cathedral](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/St_Mary's_Cathedral,_Sydney) is the cathedral church of the [Roman Catholic Archdiocese of Sydney](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roman_Catholic_Archdiocese_of_Sydney).

In 2021, Christianity was the largest religious affiliation at 46%, the largest denominations of which were [Catholicism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Catholic_Church_in_Australia) at 23.1% and [Anglicanism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anglicanism_in_Australia) at 9.2%. 30.3% of Sydney residents identified as having no religion. The most common non-Christian religious affiliations were [Islam](6037917.md) (6.3%), [Hinduism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hinduism) (4.8%), [Buddhism](3267529.md) (3.8%), [Sikhism](27964.md) (0.7%), and Judaism (0.7%). About 500 people identified with traditional Aboriginal religions.[^cite_note-Australian-Bureau-of-Statistics-15]

The [Church of England](5955.md) was the only recognised church before Governor Macquarie appointed official Catholic chaplains in 1820.[^cite_note-O'Brien-2013-351] Macquarie also ordered the construction of churches such as St Matthew's, St Luke's, St James's, and St Andrew's. Religious groups, alongside secular institutions, have played a significant role in education, health and charitable services throughout Sydney's history.[^cite_note-Religion-352]

### Crime

Main article: [Crime in Sydney](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crime_in_Sydney)

Crime in Sydney is low, with *[The Independent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Independent)* ranking Sydney as the fifth safest city in the world in 2019.[^cite_note-353] However, drug use is a significant problem. Methamphetamine is heavily consumed compared to other countries, while heroin is less common.[^cite_note-354] One of the biggest crime-related issues in recent times was the introduction of [lockout laws](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sydney_lockout_laws) in February 2014,[^cite_note-355] in an attempt to curb alcohol-fuelled violence. Patrons could not enter clubs or bars in the inner-city after 1\:30am, and last drinks were called at 3am. The lockout laws were removed in January 2020.[^cite_note-356]

## Culture

Main article: [Culture of Sydney](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Culture_of_Sydney)

### Science, art, and history



The [Art Gallery of New South Wales](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Art_Gallery_of_New_South_Wales), located in [The Domain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Domain,_Sydney), is the fourth largest public gallery in Australia.

[Ku-ring-gai Chase National Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ku-ring-gai_Chase_National_Park) is rich in [Indigenous Australian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indigenous_Australian) heritage, containing around 1,500 pieces of [Aboriginal rock art](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aboriginal_rock_art) – the largest cluster of Indigenous sites in Australia. The park's indigenous sites include [petroglyphs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Petroglyph), art sites, [burial sites](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Burial_site), caves, marriage areas, birthing areas, [midden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Midden) sites, and tool manufacturing locations, which are dated to be around 5,000 years old. The inhabitants of the area were the [Garigal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kuringgai) people.[^cite_note-357][^cite_note-discover-358] Other [rock art sites](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sydney_Rock_Art) exist in the Sydney region, such as in [Terrey Hills](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Terrey_Hills) and [Bondi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bondi,_New_South_Wales), although the locations of most are not publicised to prevent damage by vandalism, and to retain their quality, as they are still regarded as sacred sites by Indigenous Australians.[^cite_note-359]



The [State Library of New South Wales](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_Library_of_New_South_Wales) holds the oldest library collections in Australia.

The [Australian Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australian_Museum) opened in Sydney in 1827 with the purpose of collecting and displaying the natural wealth of the colony.[^cite_note-360] It remains Australia's oldest natural history museum. In 1995 the [Museum of Sydney](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Museum_of_Sydney) opened on the site of the first [Government House](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Government_House,_Sydney). It recounts the story of the city's development.[^cite_note-361] Other museums include the [Powerhouse Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Powerhouse_Museum) and the [Australian National Maritime Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australian_National_Maritime_Museum).[^cite_note-362][^cite_note-363]

The [State Library of New South Wales](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_Library_of_New_South_Wales) holds the oldest library collections in Australia, being established as the [Australian Subscription Library](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australian_Subscription_Library) in 1826.[^cite_note-History1-364] The [Royal Society of New South Wales](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Royal_Society_of_New_South_Wales), formed in 1866, encourages "studies and investigations in science, art, literature, and philosophy". It is based in a terrace house in [Darlington](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Darlington,_New_South_Wales) owned by the [University of Sydney](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Sydney).[^cite_note-365] The [Sydney Observatory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sydney_Observatory) building was constructed in 1859 and used for astronomy and meteorology research until 1982 before being converted into a museum.[^cite_note-366]

The [Museum of Contemporary Art](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Museum_of_Contemporary_Art_Australia) was opened in 1991 and occupies an [Art Deco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Art_Deco) building in [Circular Quay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Circular_Quay). Its collection was founded in the 1940s by artist and art collector John Power and has been maintained by the University of Sydney.[^cite_note-367] Sydney's other significant art institution is the [Art Gallery of New South Wales](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Art_Gallery_of_New_South_Wales) which coordinates the [Archibald Prize](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Archibald_Prize) for portraiture.[^cite_note-368] Sydney is also home to contemporary art gallery [Artspace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Artspace_Visual_Arts_Centre), housed in the historic [Gunnery Building](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Gunnery,_Woolloomooloo) in [Woolloomooloo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Woolloomooloo), fronting [Sydney Harbour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sydney_Harbour).[^cite_note-369]

### Entertainment



The [State Theatre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_Theatre_\(Sydney\)) on [Market Street](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Market_Street,_Sydney) was opened in 1929.

Sydney's first commercial theatre opened in 1832 and nine more had commenced performances by the late 1920s. The live medium lost much of its popularity to the cinema during the Great Depression before experiencing a revival after World War II.[^cite_note-370] Prominent theatres in the city today include [State Theatre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_Theatre_\(Sydney\)), [Theatre Royal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Theatre_Royal,_Sydney), [Sydney Theatre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sydney_Theatre), [The Wharf Theatre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Wharf_Theatre), and [Capitol Theatre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Capitol_Theatre,_Sydney). [Sydney Theatre Company](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sydney_Theatre_Company) maintains a roster of local, classical, and international plays. It occasionally features Australian theatre icons such as [David Williamson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_Williamson), [Hugo Weaving](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hugo_Weaving), and [Geoffrey Rush](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geoffrey_Rush). The city's other prominent theatre companies are [New Theatre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Theatre_\(Newtown\)), [Belvoir](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Belvoir_\(theatre_company\)), and [Griffin Theatre Company](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Griffin_Theatre_Company). Sydney is also home to [Event Cinemas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Event_Cinemas)' first theatre, which opened on [George St](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George_St,_Sydney) in 1913, under its former Greater Union brand; the theatre currently operates, and is regarded as one of Australia's busiest cinema locations.

The Sydney Opera House is the home of [Opera Australia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Opera_Australia) and [Sydney Symphony](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sydney_Symphony). It has staged over 100,000 performances and received 100 million visitors since opening in 1973.[^cite_note-Sydney_Opera_House-253] Two other important performance venues in Sydney are [Town Hall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sydney_Town_Hall) and the [City Recital Hall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/City_Recital_Hall). The [Sydney Conservatorium of Music](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sydney_Conservatorium_of_Music) is located adjacent to the Royal Botanic Garden and serves the Australian music community through education and its biannual Australian Music Examinations Board exams.[^cite_note-371]



A concert at the [Sydney Opera House](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sydney_Opera_House)

Many writers have originated in and set their work in Sydney. Others have visited the city and commented on it. Some of them are commemorated in the [Sydney Writers Walk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sydney_Writers_Walk) at Circular Quay. The city was the headquarters for Australia's first published newspaper, the *[Sydney Gazette](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sydney_Gazette)*.[^cite_note-372] Watkin Tench's *A Narrative of the Expedition to Botany Bay* (1789) and *A Complete Account of the Settlement at Port Jackson in New South Wales* (1793) have remained the best-known accounts of life in early Sydney.[^cite_note-373] Since the infancy of the establishment, much of the literature set in Sydney were concerned with life in the city's slums and working-class communities, notably [William Lane](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/William_Lane)'s *The Working Man's Paradise* (1892), [Christina Stead](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christina_Stead)'s *[Seven Poor Men of Sydney](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seven_Poor_Men_of_Sydney)* (1934) and [Ruth Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ruth_Park)'s *[The Harp in the South](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Harp_in_the_South)* (1948).[^cite_note-RuthPark-374] The first Australian-born female novelist, [Louisa Atkinson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Louisa_Atkinson), set several novels in Sydney.[^cite_note-375] Contemporary writers, such as [Elizabeth Harrower](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elizabeth_Harrower_\(writer\)), were born in the city and set most of their work there–Harrower's debut novel *[Down in the City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Down_in_the_City)* (1957) was mostly set in a [King's Cross](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/King's_Cross,_New_South_Wales) apartment.[^cite_note-Harrower-376][^cite_note-377][^cite_note-378] Well known contemporary novels set in the city include [Melina Marchetta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Melina_Marchetta)'s *[Looking for Alibrandi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Looking_for_Alibrandi_\(novel\))* (1992), [Peter Carey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peter_Carey_\(novelist\))'s *30 Days in Sydney: A Wildly Distorted Account* (1999), [J. M. Coetzee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/J._M._Coetzee)'s *[Diary of a Bad Year](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Diary_of_a_Bad_Year)* (2007) and [Kate Grenville](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kate_Grenville)'s *[The Secret River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Secret_River)* (2010). The [Sydney Writers' Festival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sydney_Writers'_Festival) is held annually between April and May.[^cite_note-379]

Filmmaking in Sydney was prolific until the 1920s when spoken films were introduced and American productions gained dominance.[^cite_note-380] The [Australian New Wave](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australian_New_Wave) saw a resurgence in film production, with many notable features shot in the city between the 1970s and 80s, helmed by directors such as [Bruce Beresford](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bruce_Beresford), [Peter Weir](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peter_Weir) and [Gillian Armstrong](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gillian_Armstrong).[^cite_note-381] [Fox Studios Australia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fox_Studios_Australia) commenced production in Sydney in 1998. Successful films shot in Sydney since then include *[The Matrix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Matrix)*, *[Lantana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lantana_\(film\))*, *[Mission: Impossible 2](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mission:_Impossible_2)*, *[Moulin Rouge!](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moulin_Rouge!)*, *[Star Wars: Episode II – Attack of the Clones](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Star_Wars:_Episode_II_%E2%80%93_Attack_of_the_Clones)*, *[Australia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australia_\(2008_film\))*, *[Superman Returns](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Superman_Returns)*, *[The Great Gatsby](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Great_Gatsby_\(2013_film\))* and *[Anyone but You](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anyone_but_You)*. The [National Institute of Dramatic Art](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Institute_of_Dramatic_Art) is based in Sydney and has several famous alumni such as [Mel Gibson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mel_Gibson), [Judy Davis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Judy_Davis), [Baz Luhrmann](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baz_Luhrmann), [Cate Blanchett](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cate_Blanchett), [Hugo Weaving](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hugo_Weaving) and [Jacqueline Mckenzie](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jacqueline_McKenzie).[^cite_note-382]

Sydney hosts several festivals throughout the year. The city's [New Year's Eve](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sydney_New_Year's_Eve) celebrations are the largest in Australia.[^cite_note-383] The [Royal Easter Show](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sydney_Royal_Easter_Show) is held every year at Sydney Olympic Park. [Sydney Festival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sydney_Festival) is Australia's largest arts festival.[^cite_note-384] The travelling rock music festival [Big Day Out](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Big_Day_Out) originated in Sydney. The city's two largest film festivals are [Sydney Film Festival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sydney_Film_Festival) and [Tropfest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tropfest). [Vivid Sydney](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vivid_Sydney) is an annual outdoor exhibition of art installations, light projections, and music. In 2015, Sydney was ranked the 13th top [fashion capital](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fashion_capital) in the world.[^cite_note-385] It hosts the [Australian Fashion Week](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australian_Fashion_Week) in autumn. [Sydney Mardi Gras](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sydney_Mardi_Gras) has commenced each February since 1979.

Sydney's [Chinatown](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinatown,_Sydney) has had numerous locations since the 1850s. It moved from [George Street](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George_Street,_Sydney) to Campbell Street to its current setting in Dixon Street in 1980.[^cite_note-386] Little Italy is located in Stanley Street.[^cite_note-Economy-295]

Restaurants, bars and nightclubs can be found in the entertainment hubs in the Sydney CBD ([Darling Harbour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Darling_Harbour), [Barangaroo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barangaroo,_New_South_Wales), [The Rocks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Rocks,_New_South_Wales) and [George Street](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George_Street,_Sydney)), [Oxford Street](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oxford_Street,_Sydney), [Surry Hills](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Surry_Hills), [Newtown](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Newtown,_New_South_Wales) and [Parramatta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parramatta).[^cite_note-387][^cite_note-388] [Kings Cross](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kings_Cross,_New_South_Wales) was previously considered the red-light district. [The Star](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Star,_Sydney) is the city's casino and is situated next to Darling Harbour while the new [Crown Sydney](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crown_Sydney) resort is in nearby Barangaroo.[^cite_note-389]

### Media

Main article: [Media in Sydney](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Media_in_Sydney)



Australia's national broadcaster, the [ABC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australian_Broadcasting_Corporation), is headquartered in [Ultimo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ultimo,_New_South_Wales).

The *[Sydney Morning Herald](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sydney_Morning_Herald)* is Australia's oldest newspaper still in print; it has been published continuously since 1831.[^cite_note-390] Its competitor is *[The Daily Telegraph](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Daily_Telegraph_\(Sydney\))*, in print since 1879.[^cite_note-391] Both papers have Sunday tabloid editions called *[The Sun-Herald](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Sun-Herald)* and *The Sunday Telegraph* respectively. *[The Bulletin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Bulletin_\(Australian_periodical\))* was founded in Sydney in 1880 and became Australia's longest running magazine. It closed after 128 years of continuous publication.[^cite_note-392] Sydney heralded Australia's first newspaper, the *[Sydney Gazette](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sydney_Gazette)*, published until 1842.

Each of Australia's three commercial television networks and two public broadcasters is headquartered in Sydney. [Nine's](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nine_Network) offices and news studios are in [North Sydney](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/North_Sydney,_New_South_Wales), [Ten](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Network_10) is based in [Pyrmont](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pyrmont,_New_South_Wales), and [Seven](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seven_Network) is based in [South Eveleigh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Eveleigh) in [Redfern](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Redfern,_New_South_Wales).[^cite_note-393][^cite_note-394][^cite_note-395][^cite_note-396] The [Australian Broadcasting Corporation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australian_Broadcasting_Corporation) is located in [Ultimo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ultimo,_New_South_Wales),[^cite_note-397] and the [Special Broadcasting Service](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special_Broadcasting_Service) is based in [Artarmon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Artarmon).[^cite_note-398] Multiple digital channels have been provided by all five networks since 2000. [Foxtel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foxtel) is based in [North Ryde](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/North_Ryde) and sells subscription cable television to most of the urban area.[^cite_note-399] Sydney's first [radio stations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Sydney_radio_stations) commenced broadcasting in the 1920s. Radio has managed to survive despite the introduction of television and the Internet.[^cite_note-Commercial_radio-400] [2UE](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2UE) was founded in 1925 and under the ownership of Nine Entertainment is the oldest station still broadcasting.[^cite_note-Commercial_radio-400] Competing stations include the more popular [2GB](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2GB), [ABC Radio Sydney](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ABC_Radio_Sydney), [KIIS 106.5](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/KIIS_106.5), [Triple M](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Triple_M_Sydney), [Nova 96.9](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nova_96.9) and [2Day FM](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2Day_FM).[^cite_note-401]

## Sport and outdoor activities

Main article: [Sport in Sydney](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sport_in_Sydney)

Sydney's earliest migrants brought with them a passion for sport but were restricted by the lack of facilities and equipment. The first organised sports were [boxing](4243.md), [wrestling](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wrestling), and horse racing from 1810 in [Hyde Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hyde_Park,_Sydney).[^cite_note-Sport-402] Horse racing remains popular and events such as the [Golden Slipper Stakes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Golden_Slipper_Stakes) attract widespread attention. The first cricket club was formed in 1826 and matches were played within Hyde Park throughout the 1830s and 1840s.[^cite_note-Sport-402] Cricket is a favoured sport in summer and big matches have been held at the [Sydney Cricket Ground](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sydney_Cricket_Ground) since 1878. The [New South Wales Blues](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_South_Wales_cricket_team) compete in the [Sheffield Shield](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sheffield_Shield) league and the [Sydney Sixers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sydney_Sixers) and [Sydney Thunder](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sydney_Thunder) contest the national [Big Bash](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Big_Bash_League) Twenty20 competition.

First played in Sydney in 1865, rugby grew to be the city's most popular football code by the 1880s. One-tenth of the state's population attended a New South Wales versus New Zealand rugby match in 1907.[^cite_note-Sport-402] Rugby league separated from rugby union in 1908. The [New South Wales Waratahs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_South_Wales_Waratahs) contest the [Super Rugby](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Super_Rugby) competition, while the [Sydney Rays](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sydney_Rays) represent the city in the [National Rugby Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Rugby_Championship). The national [Wallabies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wallabies_\(rugby_union\)) rugby union team competes in Sydney in international matches such as the [Bledisloe Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bledisloe_Cup), [Rugby Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Rugby_Championship), and [World Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rugby_World_Cup). Sydney is home to nine of the seventeen teams in the [National Rugby League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Rugby_League) competition: [Canterbury-Bankstown Bulldogs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canterbury-Bankstown_Bulldogs), [Cronulla-Sutherland Sharks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cronulla-Sutherland_Sharks), [Manly-Warringah Sea Eagles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manly-Warringah_Sea_Eagles), [Penrith Panthers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Penrith_Panthers), [Parramatta Eels](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parramatta_Eels), [South Sydney Rabbitohs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Sydney_Rabbitohs), [St George Illawarra Dragons](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/St_George_Illawarra_Dragons), [Sydney Roosters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sydney_Roosters), and [Wests Tigers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wests_Tigers). [New South Wales](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_South_Wales_rugby_league_team) contests the annual [State of Origin series](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_of_Origin_series) against [Queensland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Queensland_rugby_league_team).

[Sydney FC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sydney_FC) and the [Western Sydney Wanderers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_Sydney_Wanderers_FC) compete in the [A-League Men](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/A-League_Men) and [A-League Women](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/A-League_Women) competitions. The [Sydney Swans](29210.md) and [Greater Western Sydney Giants](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greater_Western_Sydney_Giants) are local [Australian rules football](2403.md) clubs that play in the [Australian Football League](4732785.md) and the [AFL Women's](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AFL_Women's). The [Sydney Kings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sydney_Kings) compete in the [National Basketball League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Basketball_League_\(Australia\)). The [Sydney Uni Flames](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sydney_Uni_Flames) play in the [Women's National Basketball League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Women's_National_Basketball_League). The [Sydney Blue Sox](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sydney_Blue_Sox) contest the [Australian Baseball League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australian_Baseball_League). The [NSW Pride](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NSW_Pride) are a member of the [Hockey One League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hockey_One). The [Sydney Bears](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sydney_Bears) and [Sydney Ice Dogs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sydney_Ice_Dogs) play in the [Australian Ice Hockey League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australian_Ice_Hockey_League). The [Swifts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_South_Wales_Swifts) are competitors in the national women's netball league.

### Major sporting venues

- Stadium Australia

  [Stadium Australia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stadium_Australia)

- Sydney Cricket Ground

  [Sydney Cricket Ground](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sydney_Cricket_Ground)

- Western Sydney Stadium

  [Western Sydney Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_Sydney_Stadium)

- Sydney Football Stadium

  [Sydney Football Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sydney_Football_Stadium_\(2022\))



Sailing on [Sydney Harbour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_Jackson)

Women were first allowed to participate in recreational swimming when separate baths were opened at [Woolloomooloo Bay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Woolloomooloo) in the 1830s. From being illegal at the beginning of the century, sea bathing gained immense popularity during the early 1900s and the first [surf lifesaving](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Surf_lifesaving) club was established at [Bondi Beach](368567.md).[^cite_note-Sport-402][^cite_note-403] [Disputes about appropriate clothing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1907_Sydney_bathing_costume_protests) for surf bathing surfaced occasionally and concerned men as well as women. The [City2Surf](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/City2Surf_\(Sydney\)) is an annual 14 km (8.7 mi) running race from the CBD to Bondi Beach and has been held since 1971. In 2010, 80,000 runners participated which made it the largest run of its kind in the world.[^cite_note-404]

Sailing races have been held on [Sydney Harbour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_Jackson) since 1827.[^cite_note-405] Yachting has been popular amongst wealthier residents since the 1840s and the [Royal Sydney Yacht Squadron](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Royal_Sydney_Yacht_Squadron) was founded in 1862. The [Sydney to Hobart Yacht Race](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sydney_to_Hobart_Yacht_Race) is a 1,170 km (727 mi) event that starts from Sydney Harbour on Boxing Day.[^cite_note-406] Since its inception in 1945 it has been recognised as one of the most difficult yacht races in the world.[^cite_note-407] Six sailors died and 71 vessels of 115 failed to finish in the 1998 edition.[^cite_note-408]



[Sydney Olympic Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sydney_Olympic_Park) was built for the [2000 Olympics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sydney_Olympics) and has become a major sporting and recreational precinct.

The [Royal Sydney Golf Club](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Royal_Sydney_Golf_Club) is based in [Rose Bay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rose_Bay,_New_South_Wales) and since its opening in 1893 has hosted the [Australian Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australian_Open_\(golf\)) on 13 occasions.[^cite_note-Sport-402] [Royal Randwick Racecourse](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Royal_Randwick_Racecourse) opened in 1833 and holds several major cups throughout the year.[^cite_note-409]

Sydney benefitted from the construction of significant sporting infrastructure in preparation for its hosting of the 2000 Summer Olympics. The Sydney Olympic Park accommodates athletics, aquatics, tennis, hockey, archery, baseball, cycling, equestrian, and rowing facilities. It also includes the high capacity [Stadium Australia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stadium_Australia) used for rugby, soccer, and Australian rules football. The [Sydney Football Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sydney_Football_Stadium_\(1988\)) was completed in 1988 and was used for rugby and soccer matches. Sydney Cricket Ground was opened in 1878 and is used for both cricket and Australian rules football fixtures.[^cite_note-Sport-402]

Sydney was one of the host cities during the [2023 FIFA Women's World Cup](45271353.md). Sydney Football Stadium and Stadium Australia were selected as venues, with the later hosting the [final](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_FIFA_Women's_World_Cup_final).[^cite_note-410]

The [Sydney International](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sydney_International) tennis tournament is held here at the beginning of each year as the warm-up for [the Grand Slam in Melbourne](97851.md). Two of the most successful tennis players in history ([Ken Rosewall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ken_Rosewall) and [Todd Woodbridge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Todd_Woodbridge)) were born in and live in the city.

Sydney co-hosted the [FIBA Oceania Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FIBA_Oceania_Championship) in 1979, 1985, 1989, 1995, 2007, 2009 and 2011.

## Government

See also: [Local government areas of New South Wales](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Local_government_areas_of_New_South_Wales)

### Historical governance



The [New South Wales parliament](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parliament_of_New_South_Wales) sits at [Parliament House](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parliament_House,_Sydney), which is the oldest public building in Australia.

The first five governors had near autocratic power in the colony of New South Wales, subject only to the laws of England and the supervision of the Colonial Office in London. Sydney was the seat of government for the colony which encompassed over half the Australian continent.[^cite_note-Kingston-2006b-411] The first Legislative Council met in 1826,[^cite_note-Kingston-2006c-412] and in 1842, the imperial parliament expanded and reformed the council, making it partly elected.[^cite_note-Hirst-2014-413] In the same year, the town of Sydney officially became a city and an elected municipal council was established.[^cite_note-414][^cite_note-Golder-1995-415] The council had limited powers, mostly relating to services such as street lighting and drainage.[^cite_note-416] Its boundaries were restricted to an area of 11.6 square kilometres, taking in the city centre and the modern suburbs of [Woolloomooloo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Woolloomooloo), [Surry Hills](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Surry_Hills), [Chippendale](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chippendale,_New_South_Wales), and [Pyrmont](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pyrmont,_New_South_Wales).[^cite_note-City-of-Sydney-2020-67] As Sydney grew, other municipal councils were formed to provide local administration.[^cite_note-417]

In 1856, New South Wales achieved responsible government with the introduction of a bicameral parliament, based in Sydney, comprising a directly elected [Legislative Assembly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_South_Wales_Legislative_Assembly) and a nominated [Legislative Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_South_Wales_Legislative_Council).[^cite_note-Kingston-2006a-418] With the federation of the Australian colonies in 1901, Sydney became the capital of the state of New South Wales and its administration was divided between the Commonwealth, State and constituent local governments.[^cite_note-Kingston-2006a-418]

### Government in the present



The [Sydney Town Hall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sydney_Town_Hall) is the seat of the [City of Sydney](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/City_of_Sydney); the oldest [local government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Local_government_areas_of_New_South_Wales) in the city

In common with other Australian capital cities, Sydney has no single local government covering its whole area. [Local government areas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Local_government_areas_of_New_South_Wales) have responsibilities such as local roads, libraries, child care, community services and waste collection, whereas the state government retains responsibility for main roads, traffic control, public transport, policing, education, and major infrastructure project.[^cite_note-419] There are 34 local government areas which are wholly or mostly within Greater Sydney as defined by the Australian Bureau of Statistics.[^cite_note-AU_Stats-2022-100][^cite_note-420] Greater Sydney's boundaries encompass [49 state electoral districts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electoral_districts_of_New_South_Wales) and [24 federal electoral districts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electorates_of_the_Australian_House_of_Representatives).[^cite_note-421]

- [Bayside](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bayside_Council)

- [Canterbury-Bankstown](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/City_of_Canterbury-Bankstown)

- [Blacktown](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/City_of_Blacktown)

- [Blue Mountains](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/City_of_Blue_Mountains)

- [Burwood](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Municipality_of_Burwood)

- [Camden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Camden_Council_\(New_South_Wales\))

- [Campbelltown](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/City_of_Campbelltown_\(New_South_Wales\))

- [Canada Bay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/City_of_Canada_Bay)

- [Inner West](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Inner_West_Council)

- [Ku-ring-gai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ku-ring-gai_Council)

- [Lane Cove](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Municipality_of_Lane_Cove)

- [Mosman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Municipality_of_Mosman)

- [Woollahra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Municipality_of_Woollahra)



[Government House](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Government_House,_Sydney) is the official residence of the [Governor of New South Wales](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Governor_of_New_South_Wales)

Sydney is the location of the secondary official residences of the [Governor-General](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Governor-General_of_Australia) and [Prime Minister](24117.md) – [Admiralty House](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Admiralty_House,_Sydney) and [Kirribilli House](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kirribilli_House) respectively.[^cite_note-422] The [Parliament of New South Wales](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parliament_of_New_South_Wales) sits in [Parliament House](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parliament_House,_Sydney) on [Macquarie Street](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Macquarie_Street,_Sydney). This building was completed in 1816 and first served as a hospital. The Legislative Council moved into its northern wing in 1829 and by 1852 had entirely supplanted the surgeons from their quarters.[^cite_note-Governor_Lachlan_Macquarie-423] Several additions have been made as the Parliament has expanded, but it retains its original [Georgian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Georgian_architecture) façade.[^cite_note-424] [Government House](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Government_House,_Sydney) was completed in 1845 and has served as the home of 25 Governors and 5 Governors-General.[^cite_note-425] The [Cabinet of Australia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cabinet_of_Australia) also meets in Sydney when needed.

The highest court in the state is the Supreme Court of New South Wales, located in Queen's Square.[^cite_note-426] The city is also the home of numerous branches of the intermediate [District Court of New South Wales](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/District_Court_of_New_South_Wales) and the lower [Local Court of New South Wales](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Local_Court_of_New_South_Wales).[^cite_note-427]

In the past, the state has tended to resist amalgamating Sydney's more populated local government areas as merged councils could pose a threat to its governmental power.[^cite_note-428] Established in 1842, the City of Sydney is one such local government area and includes the CBD and some adjoining inner suburbs.[^cite_note-429] It is responsible for fostering development in the local area, providing local services (waste collection and recycling, libraries, parks, sporting facilities), promoting the interests of residents, supporting organisations that target the local community, and attracting and providing infrastructure for commerce, tourism, and industry.[^cite_note-430] The City of Sydney is led by an elected Council and [Lord Mayor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lord_Mayor_of_Sydney).[^cite_note-431]

In federal politics, Sydney was initially considered as a [possibility for Australia's capital city](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_the_Australian_Capital_Territory#Search_for_a_capital_city_location); the newly created city of [Canberra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canberra) ultimately filled this role.[^cite_note-432] Seven Australian [prime ministers have been born in](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Australian_Prime_Ministers_by_state#Birth_places) Sydney, more than any other city, including the first prime minister, [Edmund Barton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Edmund_Barton), and the current prime minister, [Anthony Albanese](556745.md).

Essential public emergency services are provided and managed by the State Government. Greater Sydney is served by:

- [New South Wales Police Force](563673.md)

- [New South Wales Ambulance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_South_Wales_Ambulance)

- [Fire and Rescue NSW](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fire_and_Rescue_NSW)

## Infrastructure

### Education

Main article: [Education in Sydney](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Education_in_Sydney)



The [University of Sydney](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Sydney)

Education became a focus for the colony from the 1870s when public schools began to form and schooling became compulsory.[^cite_note-433] By 2011, 90% of working age residents had completed some schooling and 57% had completed the highest level of school.[^cite_note-ABSGCCSAXLS-3] 1,390,703 people were enrolled in an educational institution in 2011 with 45.1% of these attending school and 16.5% studying at a university.[^cite_note-Greater_Sydney_QuickStats-268] Undergraduate or postgraduate qualifications are held by 22.5% of working age Sydney residents and 40.2% of working age residents of the City of Sydney.[^cite_note-ABSGCCSAXLS-3][^cite_note-434] The most common fields of tertiary qualification are commerce (22.8%), engineering (13.4%), society and culture (10.8%), health (7.8%), and education (6.6%).[^cite_note-ABSGCCSAXLS-3]



The [University of Technology Sydney](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Technology_Sydney)

There are six public universities based in Sydney: The [University of Sydney](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Sydney), [University of New South Wales](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_New_South_Wales), [University of Technology Sydney](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Technology_Sydney), [Macquarie University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Macquarie_University), [Western Sydney University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_Sydney_University), and [Australian Catholic University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australian_Catholic_University). Five public universities maintain secondary campuses in the city: the [University of Notre Dame Australia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Notre_Dame_Australia), [Central Queensland University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_Queensland_University), [Victoria University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Victoria_University,_Melbourne), [University of Wollongong](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Wollongong), and [University of Newcastle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Newcastle_\(Australia\)). [Charles Sturt University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charles_Sturt_University) and [Southern Cross University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southern_Cross_University) operate secondary campuses only designated for international students. In addition, four public universities offer programs in Sydney through third-party providers: [University of the Sunshine Coast](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_the_Sunshine_Coast), [La Trobe University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/La_Trobe_University), [Federation University Australia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Federation_University_Australia) and [Charles Darwin University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charles_Darwin_University). 5.2% of residents of Sydney are attending a university.[^cite_note-Education_institution_attending-435] The University of New South Wales and the University of Sydney are ranked equal 19th in the world,[^cite_note-UNSW-Sites-436] the University of Technology Sydney is ranked in the top 100,[^cite_note-UNSW-Sites-436] while Macquarie University is ranked 237, and Western Sydney University is ranked 474.[^cite_note-437] Sydney has public, denominational, and independent schools. 7.8% of Sydney residents are attending primary school and 6.4% are enrolled in secondary school.[^cite_note-Education_institution_attending-435] There are 935 public preschool, primary, and secondary schools in Sydney that are administered by the [New South Wales Department of Education](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Department_of_Education_and_Communities_\(New_South_Wales\)).[^cite_note-438] 14 of the 17 selective secondary schools in New South Wales are based in Sydney.[^cite_note-439]

Public vocational education and training in Sydney are run by [TAFE New South Wales](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/TAFE_NSW) and began with the opening of the [Sydney Technical College](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sydney_Technical_College) in 1878.[^cite_note-Sydney_Technical_College-246] The college became the [Sydney Institute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sydney_Institute_of_TAFE) in 1992 and now operates alongside its sister TAFE facilities across the Sydney metropolitan area, namely the [Northern Sydney Institute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northern_Sydney_Institute_of_TAFE), the [Western Sydney Institute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_Sydney_Institute_of_TAFE), and the [South Western Sydney Institute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Western_Sydney_Institute_of_TAFE). At the 2011 census, 2.4% of Sydney residents are enrolled in a TAFE course.[^cite_note-Education_institution_attending-435]

### Health



The [Sydney Hospital](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sydney_Hospital), completed in 1816

The first hospital in the new colony was a collection of tents at [The Rocks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Rocks,_New_South_Wales). Many of the convicts that survived the trip suffered from [dysentery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dysentery), smallpox, [scurvy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scurvy), and [typhoid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Typhoid). Healthcare facilities remained inadequate despite the arrival of a prefabricated hospital with the [Second Fleet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Second_Fleet_\(Australia\)) and the construction of new hospitals at Parramatta, [Windsor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Windsor,_New_South_Wales), and [Liverpool](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liverpool,_New_South_Wales) in the 1790s.[^cite_note-Hospitals-440]

Governor Macquarie arranged for the construction of [Sydney Hospital](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sydney_Hospital), completed in 1816.[^cite_note-Hospitals-440] Parts of the facility have been repurposed for use as [Parliament House](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parliament_House,_Sydney) but the hospital itself still operates. The city's first emergency department was established at Sydney Hospital in 1870. Demand for emergency medical care increased from 1895 with the introduction of an ambulance service.[^cite_note-Hospitals-440] The Sydney Hospital also housed Australia's first teaching facility for nurses, the Nightingale Wing, established with the input of [Florence Nightingale](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Florence_Nightingale) in 1868.[^cite_note-ALadyDisplaced-441]

Healthcare was recognised as a right in the early 1900s and Sydney's public hospitals came under the oversight of the Government of New South Wales.[^cite_note-Hospitals-440] The administration of healthcare across Sydney is handled by eight local health districts: Central Coast, Illawarra Shoalhaven, Sydney, Nepean Blue Mountains, Northern Sydney, South Eastern Sydney, South Western Sydney, and Western Sydney.[^cite_note-442] The [Prince of Wales Hospital](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prince_of_Wales_Hospital_\(Sydney\)) was established in 1852 and became the first of several major hospitals to be opened.[^cite_note-443] [St Vincent's Hospital](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/St_Vincent's_Hospital,_Sydney) was founded in 1857,[^cite_note-Darlinghurst-184] followed by [Royal Alexandra Hospital for Children](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Royal_Alexandra_Hospital_for_Children) in 1880,[^cite_note-444] the [Prince Henry Hospital](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prince_of_Wales_Hospital_\(Sydney\)) in 1881,[^cite_note-445] the [Royal Prince Alfred Hospital](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Royal_Prince_Alfred_Hospital) in 1882,[^cite_note-446] the [Royal North Shore Hospital](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Royal_North_Shore_Hospital) in 1885,[^cite_note-447] the [St George Hospital](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/St_George_Hospital_\(Sydney\)) in 1894,[^cite_note-448] and the [Nepean Hospital](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nepean_Hospital) in 1895.[^cite_note-449] [Westmead Hospital](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Westmead_Hospital) in 1978 was the last major facility to open.[^cite_note-450]

### Transport

Main article: [Transport in Sydney](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transport_in_Sydney)

#### Roads



[Light Horse Interchange](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Light_Horse_Interchange), the largest of its kind in Australia

The motor vehicle, more than any other factor, has determined the pattern of Sydney's urban development since [World War II](32927.md).[^cite_note-Transport-451] The growth of low-density housing in the city's outer suburbs has made car ownership necessary for hundreds of thousands of households. The percentage of trips taken by car has increased from 13% in 1947 to 50% in 1960 and 70% in 1971.[^cite_note-Transport-451] The most important roads in Sydney were the nine [Metroads](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metroad), including the 110 km (68 mi) [Sydney Orbital Network](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sydney_Orbital_Network). Sydney's reliance on motor vehicles and its sprawling road network has been criticised by proponents of mass public transport and high-density housing.[^cite_note-452][^cite_note-453][^cite_note-454] The [Light Horse Interchange](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Light_Horse_Interchange) in western Sydney is the largest in the southern hemisphere.[^cite_note-455]

There can be up to 350,000 cars using Sydney's roads simultaneously during peak hour, leading to significant traffic congestion.[^cite_note-Transport-451] 84.9% of Sydney households own a motor vehicle and 46.5% own two or more.[^cite_note-Greater_Sydney_QuickStats-268] With a rate of 26.3% in 2014, Sydney has the highest utilisation of public transport for travel to work of any Australian capital.[^cite_note-456] In contrast, in 2014 only 25.2% of working residents in the City of Sydney use a car, whilst 15.8% take a train, 13.3% use a bus, and 25.3% walk.[^cite_note-457] Several significant infrastructure projects have been completed since. The CBD features a [series of alleyways and lanes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lanes_and_alleyways_of_Sydney) that provide [off-street](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alley) vehicular access to city buildings and as well as pedestrian routes through city buildings.[^cite_note-458]

#### Suburban trains

Main article: [Sydney Trains](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sydney_Trains)



[Central station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_railway_station,_Sydney) is the busiest railway station in Australia, and the city's main public transport hub.

Sydney has the largest public transport network in Australia, with 67% of residents having access to adequate public transport services.[^cite_note-459] Sydney's rail network began with the construction of a rail line to present-day Granville in 1855. Afterwards, the network grew with European settlement in the 19th century.[^cite_note-TfNSW_Trains_History-460] In the 1850s and 1860s, the rail network reached areas that are now outer suburbs of Sydney.[^cite_note-Transport-451] The first electric trains were introduced in 1926, by which point electric trams were also running. By 1991, all lines within Sydney were electrified.[^cite_note-Dictionary_of_Sydney_Electrification-461] The rail network, as well as all public transport within Sydney, is ticketed through [Opal cards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Opal_card), reusable and contactless cards that are used to automatically calculate and collect transport fares.

Established in 1906, [Central station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_railway_station,_Sydney) is the largest and busiest railway station in the state and is the main hub of the city's [rail network](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Railways_in_Sydney).[^cite_note-462] [Sydney Trains](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sydney_Trains) is the [suburban rail](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Suburban_rail) service. Its tracks form part of the New South Wales railway network. It serves 168 stations across the city and had an annual ridership of 302 million passenger journeys in 2023–24.[^cite_note-463] Sydney's railway was first constructed in 1854 with progressive extension to the network to serve both freight and passengers. The main station is the [Central railway station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_railway_station,_Sydney) in the southern part of the CBD.

#### Metro

Main article: [Sydney Metro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sydney_Metro)



[Martin Place metro station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sydney_Metro_City_&_Southwest)

The [Sydney Metro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sydney_Metro) is a [driverless](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Automatic_train_operation) [rapid transit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rapid_transit) network separate from the suburban rail network, with connections at major interchange stations. Currently, the network consists of a single line extending from [Tallawong](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tallawong) in the north-west to the city and [Sydenham](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sydenham,_New_South_Wales). This line, designated as the M1, will be extended to Bankstown in 2025 with suburbs previously on the [Bankstown railway line](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bankstown_railway_line) currently closed for conversion to the metro. A new line through the inner west to Parramatta is planned to be built by 2030.[^cite_note-464][^cite_note-465] It currently serves 21 stations. A line to serve the greater west is planned for 2026 and will include a station for the [second international airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_Sydney_Airport). Sydney metro operates with much higher frequency than the suburban rail network, with service intervals of 3–4 minutes compared to Sydney Trains' 10–15 minutes. The scheme is intended to increase Sydney's public transport capacity by 60%.[^cite_note-ANZIP_Metro-466][^cite_note-ICE_Metro-467]

#### Light rail

Main article: [Light rail in Sydney](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Light_rail_in_Sydney)



The [CBD and South East Light Rail](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CBD_and_South_East_Light_Rail) connects Sydney's CBD with the Eastern Suburbs.

Sydney once had one of the [largest tram networks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trams_in_Sydney) in the British Empire after London.[^cite_note-468] It served routes covering 291 km (181 mi). The internal combustion engine made buses more flexible than trams and consequently more popular, leading to the progressive closure of the network with the final tram operating in 1961.[^cite_note-Transport-451] From 1930 there were 612 buses across Sydney carrying 90 million passengers per annum.[^cite_note-469]

In 1997, the [Inner West Light Rail](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Inner_West_Light_Rail) opened between Central station and [Wentworth Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wentworth_Park). It ran on a [freight line](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sydney_Freight_Network) extending from the city to [Darling Harbour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Darling_Harbour) and the [Fish Markets](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fish_Markets), part of the Metropolitan Goods Lines.[^cite_note-Heritage_NSW_Metropolitan_Goods_Lines-470] The line was extended to [Lilyfield](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lilyfield) in 2000 and then [Dulwich Hill](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dulwich_Hill) in 2014. It links the [Inner West](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Inner_West) and Darling Harbour with [Central station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_railway_station,_Sydney) and facilitated 9.1 million journeys in the 2016–17 financial year.[^cite_note-471] A second, the [CBD and South East Light Rail](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CBD_and_South_East_Light_Rail) 12 km (7.5 mi) line serving the CBD and Eastern Suburbs opened in 2019–2020.[^cite_note-472] A [light rail line](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parramatta_Light_Rail) serving the western hub of [Parramatta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parramatta,_New_South_Wales) opened in 2024.[^cite_note-ABC_Parramatta_LR-473] The project is divided into two stages, with the first (the L4) replacing the [Carlingford railway line](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carlingford_railway_line) that closed in 2020. The second stage of the project, the L5, servicing the [Olympic park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sydney_Olympic_Park), is currently under construction.

#### Buses

Main article: [Buses in Sydney](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Buses_in_Sydney)

Bus services are conducted by private operators under contract to [Transport for NSW](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transport_for_NSW). [Opal cards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Opal_card) are used on bus routes as they are with all public transport in Sydney. In total, nearly 225 million boardings were recorded across the bus network.[^cite_note-474] [NightRide](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NightRide_\(bus_service\)) is a nightly bus service that operates between midnight and 5am.

#### Ferries

Main articles: [Sydney Ferries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sydney_Ferries), [List of Sydney Harbour ferries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Sydney_Harbour_ferries), and [Timeline of Sydney Harbour ferries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Timeline_of_Sydney_Harbour_ferries)



A [*Freshwater*-class ferry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Freshwater-class_ferry) at Port Jackson.

At the time the Sydney Harbour Bridge opened in 1932, the city's [ferry service](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sydney_Ferries_Limited) was the largest in the world.[^cite_note-Sydney_Ferries-475] Patronage declined from 37 million passengers in 1945 to 11 million in 1963 but has recovered somewhat in recent years.[^cite_note-Transport-451] From its hub at [Circular Quay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Circular_Quay_ferry_wharf), the [ferry network](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sydney_Ferries) extends from [Manly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manly_ferry_wharf) to [Parramatta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parramatta_ferry_wharf).[^cite_note-Sydney_Ferries-475] Ferries in sydney are operated by [Transdev Sydney Ferries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transdev_Sydney_Ferries) and operate on 10 routes across the [harbour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sydney_Harbour) and [Parramatta River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parramatta_River).

#### Airports

[Sydney Kingsford Smith Airport](266313.md) is located in [Mascot](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mascot,_New_South_Wales). It services 46 international and 23 domestic destinations.[^cite_note-Sydney_Airport_overview-30] As the busiest airport in Australia, it handled 37.9 million passengers in 2013 and 530,000 tonnes of freight in 2011.[^cite_note-Sydney_Airport_overview-30] A second airport, [Western Sydney Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_Sydney_Airport) at [Badgerys Creek](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Badgerys_Creek,_New_South_Wales) and will open in late 2026,[^cite_note-476] at a cost of \$2.5 billion.[^cite_note-477] Notably, it will not feature a [curfew](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Night_flying_restrictions), unlike Sydney Kingsford-Smith Airport, which imposes a suspension of all aircraft operations between 11 pm and 6 am. [Bankstown Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bankstown_Airport) is Sydney's second busiest airport, and serves general aviation, charter and some scheduled cargo flights. Bankstown is also the fourth busiest airport in Australia by number of aircraft movements.[^cite_note-478] Other airports include [Camden Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Camden_Airport_\(New_South_Wales\)), [The Oaks Airfield](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Oaks_Airfield), and Menangle Airfield, which are mostly used for [general aviation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_aviation), flight training[^cite_note-Camden_Airport-479] and by private operators. Seaplane terminals are located at [Rose Bay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rose_Bay_Water_Airport) and [Palm Beach](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palm_Beach_Water_Airport), and military airports at [Holsworthy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Holsworthy_Barracks) and [Richmond](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/RAAF_Base_Richmond). [Port Botany](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_Botany_\(seaport\)) has surpassed Port Jackson as the city's major shipping port. Cruise ship terminals are located at [Sydney Cove](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Overseas_Passenger_Terminal) and [White Bay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/White_Bay_Cruise_Terminal).

### Utilities



[Warragamba Dam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Warragamba_Dam) is Sydney's largest water supply dam.

Obtaining sufficient fresh water was difficult during early colonial times. A catchment called the [Tank Stream](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tank_Stream) sourced water from what is now the CBD but was little more than an open sewer by the end of the 1700s.[^cite_note-Water-480] The Botany Swamps Scheme was one of several ventures during the mid-1800s that saw the construction of wells, tunnels, steam pumping stations, and small dams to service Sydney's growing population.[^cite_note-Water-480]

The [Upper Nepean Scheme](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Upper_Nepean_Scheme) came into operation in 1886. It transports water 100 km (62 mi) from the [Nepean](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nepean_River), [Cataract](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cataract_River_\(Wollondilly\)), and [Cordeaux](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cordeaux_River) rivers and continues to service about 15% of Sydney's water needs.[^cite_note-Water-480] Dams were built on these three rivers between 1907 and 1935.[^cite_note-Water-480] In 1977 the [Shoalhaven Scheme](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shoalhaven_Scheme) brought several more dams into service.[^cite_note-481]

The state-owned corporation [WaterNSW](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WaterNSW) now manages eleven major dams: [Warragamba](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Warragamba_Dam), one of the largest domestic water supply dams in the world,[^cite_note-482] [Woronora](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Woronora_Dam), [Cataract](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cataract_Dam), [Cordeaux](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cordeaux_Dam), [Nepean](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nepean_Dam), [Avon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Avon_Dam), [Wingecarribee Reservoir](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shoalhaven_Scheme), Fitzroy Falls Reservoir, Tallowa, the [Blue Mountains Dams](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blue_Mountains_Dams), and [Prospect Reservoir](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prospect_Reservoir).[^cite_note-Dams_and_reservoirs-483] Water is collected from five catchment areas covering 16,000 km<sup>2</sup> (6,178 mi<sup>2</sup>) and total storage amounts to 2.6 TL (0.6 mi<sup>3</sup>).[^cite_note-Dams_and_reservoirs-483] The [Sydney Desalination Plant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sydney_Desalination_Plant) came into operation in 2010.[^cite_note-Water-480] WaterNSW supplies bulk water to [Sydney Water](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sydney_Water), a state-owned corporation that operates water distribution, sewerage and storm water management services.

Sydney's electricity infrastructure is maintained by [Ausgrid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ausgrid) and [Endeavour Energy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Endeavour_Energy).[^cite_note-484][^cite_note-485] Their combined networks include over 815,000 poles and 83,000 km (52,000 mi) of cables. [Submarine communications cable](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Submarine_communications_cable) systems in Sydney include the [Australia–Japan Cable](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australia%E2%80%93Japan_Cable), [Telstra Endeavour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Telstra_Endeavour) and the [Southern Cross Cable](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southern_Cross_Cable), which link Australia and countries in the Pacific.[^cite_note-486][^cite_note-487][^cite_note-488]

## Environmental issues and pollution reduction

Main article: [Environmental issues in Australia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Environmental_issues_in_Australia)

Further information: [Climate change in Australia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Climate_change_in_Australia) and [Renewable energy in Australia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Renewable_energy_in_Australia)

### Air quality



[George Street](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George_Street,_Sydney) and bushfire smoke in December 2019

As [climate change](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Climate_change_in_Australia), [greenhouse gas emissions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greenhouse_gas_emissions_by_Australia) and pollution have become a major issue for Australia, Sydney has in the past been criticised for its lack of focus on reducing pollution and emissions and maintaining [water quality](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Water_quality).[^cite_note-489] The release of the Metropolitan Air Quality Scheme (MAQS) led to a broader understanding of the causation of pollution in Sydney, allowing the government to form appropriate responses.[^cite_note-490]

The [2019–20 Australian bushfire season](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019%E2%80%9320_Australian_bushfire_season) significantly impacted outer Sydney and dramatically reduced air quality, leading to a smoky haze that lingered for days. The [air quality](10934212.md) was 11 times the [hazardous](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hazard) level in some days,[^cite_note-491][^cite_note-492] worse than New Delhi's;[^cite_note-493] it was compared to "smoking 32 cigarettes" by Brian Oliver, a respiratory diseases scientist at the [University of Technology Sydney](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Technology_Sydney).[^cite_note-494] Since Sydney is surrounded by bushland and forest,[^cite_note-495] bushfires can ring the region in a [natural phenomena](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Natural_phenomena) that is labelled "ring of fire".[^cite_note-496][^cite_note-497][^cite_note-498][^cite_note-499][^cite_note-500]

The City of Sydney became the first council in Australia to achieve formal certification as [carbon-neutral](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carbon-neutral) in 2008.[^cite_note-501][^cite_note-502] The city has reduced its 2007 carbon emissions by 6% and since 2006 has reduced carbon emissions from city buildings by up to 20%.[^cite_note-SustainableSydney2030-503][^cite_note-504] The *Sustainable Sydney 2030* program presented a guide to reducing energy in homes and offices by 30%.[^cite_note-SustainableSydney2030-503][^cite_note-505] Reductions in energy consumption have slashed energy bills by \$30 million a year.[^cite_note-506] [Solar panels](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Solar_panels) have been established on many CBD buildings to minimise carbon pollution by around 3,000 tonnes a year.[^cite_note-507]

The city also has an "[urban forest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Urban_forest) growth strategy", in which it aims to regularly increase the [tree coverage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shade_tree) in the city by frequently planting trees with strong leaf density and vegetation to provide cleaner air and create moisture during hot weather, thus lowering city temperatures.[^cite_note-508] Sydney has also become a leader in the development of [green office buildings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Green_building) and enforcing the requirement of all building proposals to be energy-efficient. The [One Central Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_Park,_Sydney) development, completed in 2013, is an example of this implementation.[^cite_note-509][^cite_note-510][^cite_note-511][^cite_note-512]

### Car-dependency



[Traffic congestion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Traffic_congestion) on the [Warringah Freeway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Warringah_Freeway), [Milsons Point](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Milsons_Point)

Australian cities are some of the most [car-dependent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Car_dependency) cities in the world,[^cite_note-513] especially by world city standards, although Sydney's is the lowest of Australia's major cities at 66%.[^cite_note-Charting_Transport-2023-514] Sydney also has the [highest usage of public transport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Modal_share) in an Australian city, at 27%–comparable with New York City, Shanghai and Berlin. Despite its high ranking for an Australian city, Sydney has a low level of public transport services, with a historically low-density layout and significant [urban sprawl](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Urban_sprawl), thus increasing the likelihood of car dependency.[^cite_note-515][^cite_note-516]

Strategies have been implemented to reduce private [vehicle pollution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Car_pollution) by encouraging public transport,[^cite_note-517] initiating the development of high density housing and introducing a fleet of 10 new [electric cars](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electric_cars), the largest order of the pollution-free vehicle in Australia.[^cite_note-518] Electric cars do not produce carbon monoxide and [nitrous oxide](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nitrous_oxide), which contribute to climate change.[^cite_note-519][^cite_note-ipcc-520] [Cycling trips](5931.md) increased by 113% across Sydney's inner-city from 2010 to 2015, at which point about 2,000 bikes were passing through top peak-hour intersections on an average weekday.[^cite_note-SustainableSydney2030-503] Transport developments in the [north-west](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sydney_Metro_Northwest) and east of the city have been designed to encourage use of the expanding public transportation system.

## Sister cities

[Sister cities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sister_city) of Sydney include:[^cite_note-521]

- [San Francisco](49728.md), United States of America

- [Wellington](33804.md), New Zealand

- [Florence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Florence), Italy

- [Nagoya](61720.md), Japan

- [Portsmouth](24207.md), United Kingdom

- [Guangzhou](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guangzhou), China
